[1024] Over the years, the participation of women in the Navy has been notable

[1025] As officers and enlisted women, they act in various areas, such as medicine, odontology, health assistance (nursing, psychology, nutrition, physiotherapy and pharmacy), engineering, architecture, civil construction, pedagogy, accounting, administration, law, history, social communication, museology, librarianship, computer science, economics, social service, security of waterway traffic, physical education, oceanography and meteorology, among other fields relevant to the Navy

[1026] Women are now military organization heads or deputy heads and team leaders

[1027] They manage projects and the construction of warship and other equipment

[1028] They lead departments, divisions and sections in the administration, in hospitals, in technology centers, in naval districts and in the Navy’s specialized directorates

[1029] In the Navy’s Medical and Engineering Corps, in particular, women may rise to the rank of General Officer (Admiral), competing in equal conditions with male officers belonging to the same Corps

[1030] On November 25, 2012, Captain Dalva Maria Carvalho Mendes, of the Navy’s Medical Corps, became the first Brazilian woman appointed to the rank of Admiral

[1031] President Dilma Rousseff greets the recently promoted Rear Admiral Dalva DEFENSE WHITE PAPER 113 CHAPTER 3 — DEFENSE AND THE MILITARY INSTRUMENT Brazilian Army The origin of the Brazilian Army (EB) dates back to the colonial period to the struggle of the colony against Dutch invaders

[1032] The first Battle of Guararapes, on April 19, 1648, is considered the genesis of the Brazilian Army

[1033] On this occasion, the Forces fighting the invaders were genuinely formed by Brazilians (white, colored and Amerindians

[1034] In 1822, the Army cooperated with the quest for independence, supporting Emperor Pedro I in a struggle that consolidated the political emancipation of the country

[1035] The Army fought, under the Empire, in conflicts of the River Plata region and contributed to the consolidation of the Brazilian territory

[1036] Led by Marshall Deodoro da Fonseca, the Brazilian Army played a fundamental role in the establishment of a Republican government in

[1037] The Army exercised a relevant role in maintaining the unity of the homeland, and it continues to do so

[1038] Its history is mixed with the country’s

[1039] The Army is present across the national territory and identifies itself with the people

[1040] In it men and women are represented without distinction of ethnic, religious, or social background

[1041] Armored units conducting maneuvers CHAPTER 3 — DEFENSE AND THE MILITARY INSTRUMENT The Country’s geopolitical complexity presents multiple scenarios for the Land Force

[1042] The Army’s mission is conditioned by a territory of continental dimensions, by geographical diversity and by a vast border with 10 countries

[1043] Mission It is the Army’s responsibility to develop the Land Force in order to meet its constitutional mission of defending the homeland, protecting the three branches of state and upholding law and order when called upon

[1044] The Army must also fulfill subsidiary roles as provided in complementary law, such as: to support national development and civil defense, as well as the country’s foreign policy and to participate in international peacekeeping and humanitarian missions

[1045] The Army integrates the National Civil Protection and Defense System (SINPDEC), taking part, across the national territory, in aid and assistance to victims of natural disasters, whether in the phase of prompt response or in the stage of recovering and reconstructing

[1046] The following subsidiary duties are also assigned to the Army: • act, through preventive or repressive actions in the land frontiers, against border and environmental crimes, separately or jointly with agencies of the Executive Branch

[1047] This includes performing patrols; screening and inspecting people, land vehicles, ships and aircraft; and arresting flagrant offenses; • cooperate with federal agencies, whenever necessary, in the repression of crime, within the national territory, by providing logistical, intelligence, communications and training support; and • cooperate with federal, state and municipal agencies on engineering services

[1048] Organization and Equipment The Army has the following organizational structure: a) One general management office, the Army Staff (EME

[1049] b) Two advisory offices: Army High Command; and Economic and Finance Council

[1050] DEFENSE WHITE PAPER CHAPTER 3 — DEFENSE AND THE MILITARY INSTRUMENT c) Six offices of direct and immediate assistance to the Army Chief of Staff: • Office of the Army Chief of Staff (Gab Cmt Ex); • Office of the Secretary-General of the Army (SGEx); • Army Intelligence Center (CIE); • Army Press Office (CComSEx); • Army Office of Internal Audit (CCIEx); and • Army Office of Legal Affairs (CJACEx

[1051] d) Seven area specific institutions: Logistics Command (COLOG); Department of Engineering and Construction (DEC); Department of Personnel (DGP); Department of Education and Culture (DECEx); Land Operations Command (COTER); Department of Science and Technology (DCT); and Economic and Finance Secretariat (SEF

[1052] The Army today has 575 military organizations distributed across the national territory

[1053] It also has 306 military shooting ranges in every region of the country

[1054] Organization chart of the Brazilian Army EME Army High Command Chief of Army Direct and Immediate Assistance Bodies General Management Office Advisory Offices Area Specific Institutions Area Commands Economic and Finance Council DGP DEC COLOG COTER DECEx DCT SEF CMO CMA CMS CML CMSE CMNE 8th RM/DE 12th RM 9th RM CMP 11th RM 3rd RM 5th RM/DE 3rd DE 6th DE 1st RM 4th RM 1st DE 6th RM 7th RM/DE 10th RM 2nd RM 2nd DE Land Force The Land Force is composed of seven Area Commands: • Amazon Command (CMA); • Western Command (CMO); • Planalto Command (CMP); • Southern Command (CMS); • Eastern Command (CML); • Southeastern Command (CMSE); and • Northeastern Command (CMNE

[1055] Western Command Southern Command Southeastern Command Eastern Command Northeastern Command Planalto Command Amazon Command Geographical distribution of area commands

[1056] The Land Force has seven Army Divisions and twelve Military Regions, some of which are cumulative

[1057] The Amazon Command and the Northeastern Command have each an engineering group

[1058] The Army Divisions incorporate brigades and artillery units

[1059] The Army has 27 brigades and four artillery units (three in the Southern Command and one in the Eastern Command

[1060] The brigades may be subordinated to the Army Divisions or directly to the Area Commands

[1061] The basic combat module is the brigade, which may be one of several types, depending on the nature of its mission, the material utilized, or the operational area to which it was designated

[1062] The brigade is a large unit based on a combined arms approach, which is able to perform missions in an autonomous manner

[1063] Normally, it is composed of: maneuverable units and sub-units (infantry and cavalry); suppressive fire units (field artillery); air defense units (anti-aircraft artillery); combat support units (engineering and communications); and logistics support units (supplies, medical services and combat materials

[1064] Brigades may receive, according to their needs, fractions of the Army’s aviation, electronic warfare, electronic intelligence, psychological operations, special operations, and anti-aircraft units, and also Unmanned Aerial Vehicles (UAVs

[1065] Brigades are characterized by the flexibility of their organization and diversity of weapons, munitions, vehicles and equipment, which, together with qualified personnel, are integrated to ensure adequate performance of complex operations

[1066] The main brigade types are: * Light Brigades: Jungle Infantry Brigade, Frontier Infantry Brigade, Motorized Infantry Brigade, Light Infantry Brigade and Parachute Infantry Brigade

[1067] The light brigades were created due to the Land Force’s need for units with great flexibility and operational capacity, which are able to move and act with celerity and efficiency anywhere in the national territory, and also to guarantee law and order, as provided in the Constitution; DEFENSE WHITE PAPER 118 CHAPTER 3 — DEFENSE AND THE MILITARY INSTRUMENT CMS CMSE CML CMP CMNE CMO CMA Cascavel Dourados Campo Grande Curitiba Ponta Grossa Florianópolis Santa Maria Porto Alegre Pelotas Bagé Uruguaiana Santiago Corumbá Cuiabá Goiânia Cristalina Brasília Rio de Janeiro Belo Horizonte Juiz de Fora Salvador Recife João Pessoa Natal Fortaleza Belém Marabá Manaus Boa Vista Tefé São Gabriel da Cachoeira Porto Velho Campinas São Paulo Taubaté Caçapava Guarujá Deployment of brigades Key Area Command Headquarters Military Division and Region (unified) Military Region Armored Brigades Mechanized Brigades Motorized Brigades Parachute Brigades Division Light Brigade/Frontiers Mountain Brigades Anti-aircraft Artillery Brigade Special Operations Brigades Army Aviation Command Engineering Group DEFENSE WHITE PAPER 119 CHAPTER 3 — DEFENSE AND THE MILITARY INSTRUMENT • Medium Brigades: Mechanized Infantry Brigades and Mechanized Cavalry

[1068] Medium Brigades have armored vehicles on wheels

[1069] They are assigned for defense operations against foreign threats

[1070] During conflicts, they may be used for scouting and security purposes

[1071] They delay potential aggressors until the Land Force deploys troops which are in better conditions to face the enemy; and • Heavy Brigades: Armored Cavalry Brigade and Armored Infantry Brigade

[1072] The Heavy Brigades are armored brigades

[1073] They are potent, highly mobile forces and, therefore, can be decisive in land conflicts

[1074] Their mission is to close in on the enemy, in order to destroy or neutralize, utilizing fire, maneuver and shock tactics

[1075] In the battlefield, they are particularly capable of conducting highly mobile offensive actions with great depth

[1076] There are also three specific brigade combat modules: • Army Air Command; • Special Operations Brigade; and • Anti-aircraft Artillery Brigade

[1077] The strategic conception of employment and the operational environment indicate the nature, organization and material which are to be used by the Land Force

[1078] From this premise, brigades are organized and trained to act mainly in their areas of specialization

[1079] In addition to human resources, the Land Force possesses the following equipment, in order to meet its Constitutional mission: A combat module of a Brigade is the lowest ranking unit of the Land Force which gathers under a single command the main operational systems (maneuver, intelligence, command and control, fire suppression, mobility, counter-mobility and protection, anti-aircraft defense and logistics), allowing combined training of arms and services

[1080] This combination must integrate combat units (infantry and cavalry), combat support units (engineering, artillery and communications) and logistics units, with specific tasks, which compose the operational systems

[1081] The combat module is able to act independently and to endure in action

[1083] Military manpower, which is compatible with the Country’s political and strategic stature, is essential

[1084] The Army develops capabilities, whether jointly with the other Forces or separately, to meet three requirements simultaneously: secure the defense of the territory, project power in order to secure vital interests and meet foreign policy demands for security, international peace and regional integration

[1085] These capabilities require Forces at a high level of readiness, supported by others formed by the mobilization of material and human resources

[1086] Based on lessons learned from contemporary wars and trends of future conflicts, the Army develops its resources based on capabilities, since this approach provides the institution with effective tools to respond to diffuse challenges in future

[1087] DEFENSE WHITE PAPER CHAPTER 3 — DEFENSE AND THE MILITARY INSTRUMENT

[1088] The following new capabilities are considered priorities for the Army: • land deterrence compatible with the country’s political and strategic status; • international projection of the Army in support of Brazil’s foreign policy; • performance in cyberspace with freedom of action; • Land Force logistics readiness; • interoperability (with other Forces) and complementarity (with other agencies); • integrated management at all levels; • effective military doctrine; • higher emphasis on the human dimension; • adequate budget; • defense products linked to operational capacity; and • systemic management of operational data

[1089] Strategic Vision and Goals The Army is a public and permanent national institution

[1090] It is a fundamental instrument for the security and defense of the Brazilian state

[1091] The internal and external acknowledgement of the Army’s capacity to fulfill its mission demonstrates the Force’s relevance to the society it serves

[1092] Such relevance must be permanently sustained through the understanding of the people’s needs, of the state’s demands and the conditions imposed by the environment

[1093] The Army has sought to modernize its equipment and armament, as well as the quality of its personnel

[1094] However, to meet strategic demands, it has become evident that, beyond adapting and modernizing, the Army must adopt the concept of transformation

[1095] Transformation refers to the development of capabilities to comply with new duties, whether derived from the present operational environment or from future challenges

[1096] This concept is detailed in chapter

[1097] Consistent with the dynamic development of the strategic environment, the Army started a Transformation Process which will be continuous and oriented by a Force Project

[1098] Both Process and Project are inserted into the Planning System of the Army

[1099] The Army Staff completed a diagnostic of the Land Force, based on the National Defense Strategy, and proposed actions for its adaptation to new demands of the state and the Brazilian people

[1100] This initiative has resulted in the Strong Arm Strategy (EBF

[1101] DEFENSE WHITE PAPER CHAPTER 3 — DEFENSE AND THE MILITARY INSTRUMENT The following premises, established by the National Defense Strategy, were considered in the preparation of the Strong Arm Strategy: • the Army will be organized under the aegis of the trinomial monitoring/control, mobility and presence; • it must have strategic mobility to respond quickly to any threat or aggression; • it must deploy its strategic reserves in a way that allows for fast concentration and employment of Forces

[1102] • it must deepen the presence of military units in the frontiers; • it must develop the concept of flexibility in combat in order to meet monitoring/control, mobility and presence requirements; • it must develop the principle of elasticity, which is the capacity to quickly increase the quantity of troops; and • the Amazon region is of great interest for the Ministry of Defense and the Army must be able to deploy equipment and personnel in this area

[1103] The Strong Arm Strategy is composed of a Deployment Plan, which determines the positioning of personnel in order to meet the strategic concepts of employment, presence and deterrence, and to attend to requirements of an Equipment Plan

[1104] The former comprises the Protected Amazon Program and the Homeland Sentinel Program

[1105] The latter comprises the Strategic Mobility Program and the Brazilian Combatant Program

[1106] These plans are aligned to the Defense Deployment and Equipment Plan (PAED

[1107] The Strong Arm Strategy therefore comprehends the following programs: MODERNIZATION CRITICAL AREAS TRANSFORMATION/ STRENGTH PROJECT DEPLOYMENT PROTECTED AMAZON HOMELAND SENTINEL EQUIPMENT STRATEGIC MOBILITY BRAZILIAN COMBATANT CORE ACTIVITY MANAGEMENT PERSONNEL DOCTRINE PREPARATION LOGISTICS ENGINEERING EDUCATION HUMAN RESOURCES MANAGEMENT PROCESS/PRODUCTS BUDGET AND FUNDING MEM AND SCIENCE &TECHNOLOGY EMPLOYMENT STRENGTH PROJECT Army’s Transformation Project DEFENSE WHITE PAPER 126 CHAPTER 3 — DEFENSE AND THE MILITARY INSTRUMENT • Protected Amazon Program — a set of projects aimed at strengthening the presence of the Land Force in the Amazon

[1108] It includes the progressive installation of new Special Frontier Platoons, as well as the modernization of existing ones

[1109] In a second stage, the Program determines actions for the strengthening of the operational and logistics structure of the Amazon Command

[1110] Simultaneously, the Army will implement a Border Monitoring System (SISFRON), through the use of electronic and radar means, such as the SABER M60 and others to be developed, in integration with other military and civilian systems, with the purpose of optimizing the capacity of monitoring around 16 thousand kilometers of border area in the Amazon, Midwest and Southern regions; • Homeland Sentinel Program — includes the reorganization, the improvement and, especially, the integration of logistics and operational structures of the area commands

[1111] As aforementioned, the Amazon Command is contemplated in the Protected Amazon Program; Strategic Mobility Program — aims to meet the Army’s needs in terms of replacements and material modernization, in order to improve mobility, flexibility and interoperability with other Armed Forces; and Brazilian Combatant Program — includes projects dedicated to the research and development of Defense products, the priority being the growth of nationalization and acquisitions in the internal market

[1112] Based on the aforementioned strategy and programs, priority projects were selected with the purpose of transforming the Land Force by supplying brigades with means of transportation, equipment, armaments and provisions, in order to meet demands and aspired levels of modernization

[1113] These projects include: Recovery of Land Force’s Operational Capacity (RECOP); Cyber Protection System — Cyber Defense; Integrated Border Monitoring System (SISFRON); Integrated System for the Protection of Strategic Land Structures (PROTEGER); New Family of National Armored Vehicles (Guarani); Antiaircraft Defense System; and Missiles and Rockets System (ASTROS 2020), detailed in chapter 5. Education — Main Training Centers Army Academy (AMAN

[1114] The Army Academy originated in 1792, with the creation of the Royal Academy of Artillery, Fortification and Design, in Rio de Janeiro — the first military school of the Americas

[1115] During the 19th century, the name was successively changed to Royal Military Academy, Imperial Military Academy and Military Academy of the Royal Court

[1116] From 1906 to 1910, this institution functioned at the War School in Porto Alegre (State of Rio Grande do Sul), and in 1913, at the Military School of Realengo (State of Rio de Janeiro

[1117] It was only on January 1st, 1944 that the Army Academy was installed in a permanent site in Resende (State of Rio de Janeiro

[1118] In 1951, the institution was officially designated the name of Army Academy

[1119] The Academy develops the Army’s career combat officers

[1120] The undergraduate program activities are carried out to develop necessary qualities for the military profession

[1121] The Program lasts five years

[1122] The first year is undertaken in the Army Cadets Preparatory School (EsPCEx), located in Campinas (State of São Paulo

[1123] The remaining four years are studies at the Army Academy itself

[1124] The curriculum includes subjects related to military and exact sciences, as well as humanities

[1125] Upon concluding the program, the graduate is declared officer candidate and is granted a military sciences degree

[1126] Admittance to the Army Academy occurs exclusively through national competitive exams for the Army Cadets Preparatory School

[1127] The founder of the Army Academy was Marshal José Pessoa Cavalcanti de Albuquerque, who chose the location for the new building, conceived the project for the institution and reinstated the title of “cadet”, which had been abandoned at the time of the proclamation of the Republic

[1128] He also determined the use of historic uniforms and created the “Short Sword of the Duke of Caxias”

[1129] The Army Academy programs aim to develop basic military character, with solid ethics and strong qualities, including leadership, initiative, discipline, responsibility and teamwork

[1130] Throughout the teaching process, the cadet develops humanistic, scientific and technological knowledge necessary for a career in the Army

[1131] In the Academy, the cadet acquires a technical and military foundation, through training and exercises, in order to be able, as a future officer, to exert command and leadership of small groups (platoons and sections) and of subunits (companies, batteries or squads

[1132] Junior Officer School (Es AO) Founded in April 8, 1920, by the Minister of War Dr. João Pandiá Calógeras, the Junior Officer School has the mission of developing captains, enabling them to command and lead military units and preparing them for the exercise of staff duties which are not reserved for the active duty Army Staff group

[1133] The program offered by this School lasts two years

[1134] The first year is undertaken through distance learning, the second year requires class attendance

[1135] Army Academy — Resende (State of Rio de Janeiro

[1136] Command and Staff College (ECEME) Created in 1905 and located in Rio de Janeiro, the Command and Staff College is a traditional Brazilian Army education institution

[1137] Its mission is to prepare senior ranking officers for staff, command, leadership, management and advisory roles in the highest ranks of the Land Force

[1138] The College also cooperates with general and specialised organisations in the Army in the field of doctrine for force development and employment

[1139] All Command and Staff College programs are of graduate level and taught in accordance with the country’s legislation on university education and the Army’s education regulations

[1140] The Command and Staff College is increasing its relationship with similar military schools in the Navy (Naval War College), in the Air Force (Air Force Command and Staff College) and with civilian universities

[1141] Currently, it also accepts in its programs military personnel from friendly nations, mainly from South America and Africa

[1142] Located in the city of Rio de Janeiro, the Military Engineering Institute originated from the merger of the Army’s Technical School and the Military Institute of Technology, in

[1143] The Military Engineering Institute has the mission of developing officers for the Army’s Military Engineering Group (QEM

[1144] The following programs are offered: undergraduate degree, exclusively for officers from the Army Academy; undergraduate degree and basic military training for young civilians who finished high school; and basic training program for graduate engineers who wish to join the Army

[1145] It also offers Masters and PhD degrees for civilian and military personnel

[1146] Furthermore, the Institute plans, coordinates, controls and executes competitive examinations for admittance and the selective recruitment process for the Army’s Military Engineering Group

[1147] The quality of the Institute’s programs is apparent from the excellent results which it regularly obtains in the Programs General Index (IGC

[1148] It is always among the 14 best institutions, out of over two thousand participants

[1149] The Army Technological Project determines the transfer of the Military Engineering Institute from its current facilities to Guaratiba (State of Rio de Janeiro), near the Army Technology Center (CTEx

[1150] New and modern buildings, with state-of-the-art equipment, and proximity to civilian and military research and development organization will allow the Institute to reach higher levels of excellence in its activities

[1151] The study on IME transfer and updating process is in a conclusion stage

[1152] The Programs General Index (IGC) evaluates the performance of university level institutions in the country

[1153] It synthesizes in one index the quality of all undergraduate, master's and PhD degrees in an educational institution

[1154] The Index is published by the Anísio Teixeira National Institute for Educational Studies and Research (Inep) of the Ministry of Education, immediately after the results of the National Students Performance Exam (Enade) are published

[1155] Army Health School (EsSEx

[1156] The Army Health School was created in 1910 with the name of Military Applied Medical School

[1157] Located in the city of Rio de Janeiro (RJ), the School is a university level educational institution in the field of military health education

[1158] Its mission is to: develop active duty officers of the Medical Doctors’ Group of the Army’s Health Service; coordinate graduate programs for Health Service officers, as well as other programs for military personnel of the Complementary Group in the fields of Nursing, Veterinary Science and Psychology; contribute to the development of military doctrine in the health area; carry out research in Health Studies, in cooperation with similar institutions; offer internships in the health area; and administer competitive examinations for admittance into military health programs

[1159] Army School of Administrative and Technical Studies The Army Administrative and Technical School was created on April 5, 1988 with the name of Army School of Administration

[1160] Located in the city of Salvador (BA), the School began its activities by offering programs for Army career officers and enlisted men

[1161] On October 2, 1989, Law

[1162] Law 7,831 created the Complementary Officers Group to meet changes required by the modernization of the institution

[1163] From then on, the School took charge of the education of officers for this new Group

[1164] The School currently develops men and women with previous degrees in different fields from university level institutions acknowledged by the Ministry of Education

[1165] Applicants to the School must pass competitive exams, of national scope

[1166] Once admitted, they then attend the Program for Officers of the Complementary Group (CFO/QC) which enables them to perform and assume responsibilities and roles of Army officers, within their field of expertise

[1167] On November 8, 2010, the Ministerial Regulation No 1,080 transformed the Army School of Administration into the Army School of Administrative and Technical Studies, changing its mission and organization

[1168] Besides officers of the Complementary Group, the School began, from then on, to develop officers in the professions of dentistry and pharmacy

[1169] Combat Sergeant School (EsSA) Located in the city of Três Corações (State of Minas Gerais), the Combat Sergeant School is the military educational institution in charge of selecting and educating sergeants in the professions of Infantry, Cavalry, Artillery, Engineering and Communications

[1170] The program, which lasts one year and a half, develops students so that they are able to exercise roles in military organizations

[1171] Logistics Sergeant School (EsSLog) Located in the city of Rio de Janeiro, this School was established in 2010 due to the transformation of the School of Military Materiel

[1172] It is the military educational institution responsible for selecting and educating sergeants in the professions of supply management, communication maintenance, vehicles maintenance, weapons maintenance, topography and music

[1173] The School’s main purpose is the professional development of the Army’s logistics sergeant

[1174] All curriculum activities seek to enable students to exercise specialized roles within Army organizations

[1175] Senior Combat Sergeant School (EASA

[1176] The Senior Combat Sergeant School was established on July 10, 1992, with the name of Sergeant Training Center — South, subordinated to the Southern Command, in the quarters of the 17th Infantry Battalion

[1177] The School has the mission of further educating sergeants of the Brazilian Army

[1178] To achieve this purpose, it offers the Further Studies Program for Sergeants (CAS) of the Infantry, Cavalry, Artillery, Engineering and Communications professions, thereby enabling them to exercise roles at their rank level, including in administrative areas

[1179] Scientific and Technological Institutions Army Technology Center (CTEx) Established in 1979, in Rio de Janeiro, the Army Technology Center is a military organization directly subordinated to the Department of Science and Technology

[1180] Its mission is to conduct research and development of defense products of interest to the Land Force

[1181] To accomplish this goal, the Center, on occasion, engages in partnerships with national companies for the joint development of products and receives financial support from the Studies and Projects Fund (FINEP) of the Ministry of Science, Technology and Innovation (MCTI

[1182] These partnerships were celebrated, for example, to develop the Saber M60 radars

[1183] Among the projects under development by the Army Technology Center, the following are noteworthy

[1184] • radar SABER M60 — the project contemplates a low altitude anti-aircraft defense system, which protects sensitive points and areas; • anti-vehicle light weapon — developed by the Army Technology Center in partnership with national companies, it is a weapon of individual use for anti-vehicle combat at close quarters; • missile MSS 1.2 AC — has the objective of attending to the demands of modern combat, including high level of mobility, strong firepower and the capacity to engage different types of targets; and • flight simulator for Esquilo and Fennec helicopters — a product of the Army Technology Center, developed in partnership with a national company, it aims to train Army pilots

[1185] Foreign Exchange and Cooperation The Army maintains a series of exchange and international cooperation activities with other nations

[1186] The Land Force’s growing cooperation with South American and African countries is noteworthy, particularly the BrazilParaguay Military Cooperation, which began in 1942, originally named Brazilian Military Training Mission in Paraguay (MMBIP

[1191] Maria Quiteria is considered to be the first Brazilian woman to enlist in a military unit

[1192] However, it was only in 1943, during World War II, that women were officially admitted into the Brazilian Army

[1193] In that year 73 nurses (67 of them based in hospitals and six specialized in air transport) were recruited

[1194] These volunteers served in four different United States Army hospitals

[1195] After the war, as with the rest of the Brazilian Expeditionary Force (FEB), the majority of nurses were awarded medals, promoted to officer ranks and placed on leave from regular military service

[1196] In 1992, the Army Administration School in Salvador (BA) enrolled the Army’s first student cohort which included women — 49 in total, approved in competitive examinations

[1197] The Army instituted, in 1996, the female volunteer military service for medical doctors, dentists, pharmacists, veterinarians and nurses with undergraduate degrees

[1198] In the same year, the first class of 290 volunteer women was incorporated into the Army to render military service in the healthcare area

[1199] In 1997 the Military Engineering Institute enrolled its first 10 female students into the Military Engineers’ Group (QEM

[1200] Also in 1997, the Army Health School (EsSEx) enrolled and educated its first cohort of university-level female medical doctors, dentists, pharmacists, veterinarians and nurses for the Army Officer Health Group

[1201] In 1998, the Army instituted a technical traineeship for graduates from other fields

[1202] In that year, to meet institutional demands, it incorporated the first cohort of female lawyers, business administrators, accountants, teachers, systems analysts, engineers, architects, journalists, among other professionals, totaling 519 women

[1203] FUERZAS COMANDO COMPETITION El Salvador Dominican Republic Bahamas, Belize, Brazil, Chile, Colombia, Costa Rica, El Salvador, Ecuador, USA, Guatemala, Honduras, Nicaragua, Paraguay, Panama, Peru, Dominican Republic and Uruguay. Multinational competition involving special forces from countries in the American continent, with alternating venues

[1204] Its objective was to develop and maintain alliances and regional cooperation; strengthen political and military relations and improve training, interoperability and the capacity of the special forces for implementing tactics, techniques and procedures in their field of expertise

[1205] The Health School, in 2001, opened applications for women to participate in competitive examinations for positions in the Health Sergeant Program

[1206] In 2011 the Army Command and Staff College awarded, for the first time since its establishment, certificates to three female officers

[1207] The female officers (and medical doctors) concluded the Leadership and General Staff Program for officers of the Health Group

[1208] They therefore have the possibility to compete, in future, for promotion to General Officer rank

[1209] The Army’s greatest asset is motivated men and women, who are able to fulfill their missions

[1210] Therefore, in order to correct a legal gap, the Executive Branch has proposed a bill to allow women to be admitted into more military programs which develop Army career personnel, taking into account the peculiarities of military life

[1211] The Brazilian Air Force (FAB) has its origins in the aviation of the Brazilian Navy and the Brazilian Army

[1212] In 1916 the Navy founded the Naval Aviation School, in the Island of Enxadas, in the city of Rio de Janeiro

[1213] In 1919, the Army created the Military Aviation School at Campo dos Afonsos, a neighborhood of the same city

[1214] In 1941, during World War II, the Ministry of Aeronautics was created from the merger of aerial and human resources from the Army, Navy and Civil Aviation Department

[1215] Its armed branch was initially called National Air Forces, and in June of the same year, it became called Brazilian Air Force

[1216] It is noteworthy that, in 1932, the first flight of the Military Air Mail (CAM) departed from Campo dos Afonsos

[1217] This service was later replaced by the National Air Mail (CAN), which has been of great importance for national integration

[1218] The first Minister of the Air Force was a lawyer and political leader named Joaquim Pedro Salgado Filho

[1219] Aircraft A-1 The Air Force had its baptism of fire during World War II

[1220] It took part in the anti-submarine campaign of the South Atlantic and in air combat over Italy, on the side of the Allies

[1221] With the establishment of the Ministry of Defense in 1999, the Ministry of Aeronautics, similarly to the other Armed Services, was subordinated to this new ministry and renamed Brazilian Air Force

[1222] The challenges faced by the Brazilian Air Force are proportional to the country’s dimensions

[1223] The continental dimensions of the Brazilian airspace which needs to be patrolled, controlled and defended, as well as the complexity of the international environment, generate multiple demands for the Air Force, which in turn requires modern and efficient means to meet these challenges

[1224] Mission The Air Force is responsible for maintaining sovereignty over the national airspace in order to defend the homeland

[1225] In particular, it must stop any acts hostile to national interests

[1226] To achieve its purpose, the Air Force relies on its capacity for surveillance, control, and defense of the national airspace, through resources of detection, interception and destruction

[1227] The Air Force has the following subsidiary roles: • provide security for airspace navigation; • establish, equip and operate aerospace, aerial and airport infrastructure under its responsibility; • operate the National Air Mail; • cooperate with federal agencies in the area of law enforcement involving the use of airspace and airport areas, through the provision of logistics, intelligence, communications and training; and • act in a continuous and permanent manner, alongside the judicial police services, through airspace control, against all types of illicit air traffic, especially the traffic of drugs, weapons, ammunition and illegal passengers

[1228] For the performance of this last role, the Air Force acts in cooperation with inspection agencies, which take action after landing of aircraft involved in illegal air traffic

[1229] In the absence of these agencies, the Air Force may inspect people, land vehicles, boats and aircraft, as well as arrest individuals engaged in flagrant infractions

[1230] Considering the nature of these roles, the Air Force Chief of Staff is officially designated “Military Air Authority”

[1231] The Air Force, as provided by law and whenever authorized by the President of the Republic or by his delegate, has the power to destroy any aircraft classified as hostile after all legal coercive means have been exhausted, as provided in Law

[1232] Law No 7,565 of December 19, 1986, altered by Law

[1233] No 9,614 of March

[1234] The Air Force has the following organizational structure: a) One general management office, the Air Staff (EMAER

[1235] b) Two senior advisory offices: • Air Force High Command; and • High Council of Economics and Finance

[1236] c) Eight offices of direct and immediate assistance to the Air Force Chief of Staff: • Office of the Air Force Chief of Staff (GABAER); • Air Force Intelligence Center (CIAER); • Air Force Press Office (CECOMSAER); • Air Force Accident Investigation and Prevention Center (CENIPA); • Air Force Office for Congressional Relations (ASPAER); • Air Force Institute of Air History and Culture (INCAER); • Air Force Committee for Officers’ Promotion (CPO); and • Airspace Office for Operational Security and Airspace Control (ASOCEA

[1237] d) Seven area specific offices: • Air Operations Command (COMGAR); • Support Command (COMGAP); • Personnel Command (COMGEP); • Office of the Secretary of Economics and Finance (SEFA); • Department of Airspace Control (DECEA); • Department of Aerospace Science and Technology (DCTA); and • Department of Education (DEPENS

[1238] Air Operations Command The Air Operations Command (COMGAR) is responsible for the preparation and employment of the Air Force’s main resources

[1239] This organization’s roles include commanding, planning, directing, controlling, coordinating, executing and assessing the employment of all air units

[1240] Seven large Regional Air Commands (COMAR) are subordinated to the Air Operations Command

[1241] The Regional Air Commands are administrative organs of the Air Force

[1242] They support air units in their respective geographical areas, with the objective of ensuring effective employment of air power in real or training missions

[1243] The seven Regional Air Commands have their headquarters in the cities of Belém, Recife, Rio de Janeiro, São Paulo, Canoas, Brasília and Manaus

[1244] The commander of a Regional Air Command is responsible for issues concerning the Air Force in his respective area of jurisdiction

[1245] The administrative structure of a Regional Air Command encompasses all fields of action of the Air Force

[1246] The Air Force is also organized in operational commands called Air Forces, also subordinated to the Air Operations Command

[1247] They are in charge of preparing and employing aircraft of various types and in different operational environments

[1248] Currently, the Fourth Air Force (IV FAE) and the Sixth Air Force (VI FAE) are deactivated

[1249] Organization chart of the Air Force EMAER Air Force High Command Air Force Commander Direct and Immediate Assistance Offices General Management Office Senior Advisory Office Area Specific Institutions Regional Air Commands Economy and Finance Superior Council II COMAR I COMAR IV COMAR V COMAR VI COMAR VII COMAR III COMAR DEPENS COMGEP COMGAR DECEA COMGAP SEFA DCTA • First Air Force (I FAE) — trains Air Force pilots in fighter, helicopter, transport, reconnaissance and patrol aviations; • Second Air Force (II FAE) — employs aircraft in air tactical operations independently or jointly with the other Forces and in search and rescue missions; • Third Air Force (III FAE) — employs strategic and tactical fighters as well as reconnaissance and aerial defense aircraft; and • Fifth Air Force (V FAE) — is responsible for transport, flight refueling, parachute launching and support units as well as for supporting Army units

[1257] Airbus A-320 Presidential airplane 1 Photo VC-2 Embraer 190 Presidential airplane 2 Photo Capabilities The Air Force currently possesses approximately 67 thousand military personnel

[1258] Its equipment is classified by type of employment, i.e. fighter, patrol, transport, search & rescue, rotor-wing and reconnaissance aircraft

[1259] This materiel is distributed in air bases across the national territory

[1260] The air bases are in charge of providing the necessary administrative, logistical, operational and security support to the Air Force and the operational commands that act within their premises, permanently or temporarily

[1261] The following factors are considered important for the full utilization of the Air Force’s capabilities to achieve the institution’s mission

[1262] In the strategic realm: • capacity to visualize areas of crisis or conflict; • technological capacity; and • institutional and international relations

[1263] In the logistics realm: • combined and joint operational capacity as well as continuous logistical support; • security and redundancy of communications system; • maintenance of elevated morale; • human, financial and material resources; • information technology training and education; and • organizational structure, asset management and administrative rationality

[1264] In the operational realm: • command and control capacity; • combat capacity; • capacity to operate in hostile electromagnetic environments; • capacity for self-defense on land and against aircraft; • intelligence capacity; and • operational planning capacity

[1265] CHAPTER 3 — DEFENSE AND THE MILITARY INSTRUMENT Strategic Vision and Goals To fulfill the Air Force’s mission — as provided in the Constitution and federal laws, and according to the National Defense Strategy — an Institutional Planning System was established

[1266] This process defines a logical sequence of procedures so as to ensure unity of thought and action, which are indispensable for operational efficiency and administrative continuity within the Air Force

[1267] The following diagram shows the strategic objectives of the Air Force, set in Air Force’s Strategic Military Plan — PEMAER

[1268] Strategic Objectives STRATEGIC OBJECTIVES BRAZILIAN SOCIETY PREPARATION AND EMPLOYMENT INTERNAL PROCESS PEOPLE & GROWTH Development and Employment Achieve excellence in airspace control, under the responsibility of SISCEAB Achieve excellence of operational capacity in the Air Force Optimize the organizational management of the Air Force Maximize the allocation of strategic budgetary and financial resources for the Air Force Amplify the scientific and technological capacity of the Air Force Improve support for military and civilian personnel in the Air Force Modernize training and education systems Integrate itself permanently into the Brazilian society’s defense mentality Enable the country to develop and construct aerospace vehicles Seek to strengthen the Brazilian aerospace and defense industries Secure sovereignty of national airspace The System allows the Air Force to: set a medium to long-term vision; manage a process oriented towards results; integrate planning, budgeting and management; and promote the conceptual alignment of documents

[1269] The Air Force’s Strategic Military Plan (PEMAER) is a product of the Planning System

[1270] This Plan reaffirms projects and actions contained in the Defense Deployment and Equipment Plan (PAED), sets the objectives for the Air Force for an intended period and guides the prioritization process of budget resources, while defining duties and responsibilities

[1271] Based on a methodology developed by the Air Force, a strategy for planning and action was conceived, which sets three structural axes described below

[1272] Strengthening operational capacity This axis has the following main objectives: supply the necessary means for the safe and efficient circulation of both civilian and military air traffic in Brazilian airspace; and contribute to the defense of national sovereignty, through aircraft development, acquisition and modernization

[1273] Main benefits: • effective capacity for surveillance, control and defense of airspace over vulnerable points and areas of the national territory, based on resources for detection, interception and destruction; • stronger military capacity to integrate main efforts for defending the nation

[1274] Actions related to Air Force re-equipment would be prioritized; and • assimilation of knowledge which will allow the country to achieve technological independence in the airspace sector

[1275] Scientific-technological training and education Planning regarding the aerospace scientific-technological complex is guided by the continual effort to educate and train human resources, as well as acquire physical capital, in order to attain full knowledge over the technologies required

[1276] To achieve this purpose, the Air Force allocates special attention to research as well as technological and industrial development in the aerospace sector

[1277] Main benefits: • contribution to the reduction of foreign dependence, in straight cooperation with national industry, especially in the aerospace sector, through the gradual nationalization of equipment and services; • adequate plans to support national industry by promoting associations, participations and acquisitions that secure competitiveness, with the aim of promoting exports; and • human resources development in the field of Air Force related technology

[1278] Organizational and operational transformation The Air Force transformation process seeks to identify the ideal dimensions and structure for the organization, in order to eliminate strategic vulnerabilities, and thereby allow the Air Force to carry out its mission of National Defense and of promoting the country’s development

[1279] Main benefits: • full knowledge of advanced technologies, through the modernization of human resources education and training systems; • maximization of operational and management effectiveness within the Air Force, in accordance with new operational capacities and concepts, which require organizational change; • appreciation of Air Force professionals through the improvement of support structures; and • promotion of relations that expedite planning and interoperability with other Armed Services, and that favors the deployment, employment and support to the Air Force

[1280] Education - Main Institutions Air Force Academy (AFA) The Air Force Academy replaced the Aeronautics School, an institution originally located in Campo dos Afonsos, which developed aviation and logistics officers for the Air Force since the creation of the Ministry of Aeronautics in

[1281] The name changed from Aeronautics School to Air Force Academy in

[1282] In 1971, the Academy was transferred to new facilities in Pirassununga (State of São Paulo

[1283] It is now responsible for developing cadets through the Pilot Officers Program (CFOAV), the Logistics Officers Program (CFOINT) and the Infantry Officers Program (CFOINF

[1284] These undergraduate degree programs last four years and prepare officers for the initial ranks of the Air Force in the aviation, logistics and infantry careers

[1285] The majority of first-year vacancies are filled by students from the Air Cadets Preparatory School (EPCAr), a high school level Air Force institution located in Barbacena (State of Minas Gerais), which offers a three-year program

[1286] The remaining places are filled through an open competitive selection process

[1287] On concluding fourth year, the cadet is declared an officer candidate and is granted a Bachelor of Science in Aeronautical Science

[1288] Air Force University (UNIFA) Established in 1983 and located in the city of Rio de Janeiro, the Air Force University is linked to the Air Force Department of Education (DEPENS

[1289] It has the purpose of planning, guiding, coordinating and controlling programs aimed at further studies and advanced military studies, which are required for preparing officers for the highest ranks

[1290] The University also offers Master’s and PhD degrees as well as graduate certificates and internships

[1291] The Air Force Command and General Staff College (ECEMAR) and the Air Force Junior Officer School (EAOAR) are subordinated to the University

[1292] Air Force Command and Staff College (ECEMAR

[1293] The Air Force Command and Staff College was instituted in 1947, in Rio de Janeiro

[1294] It offers advanced studies programs to Air Force senior officers

[1295] The College is in charge of updating student knowledge on aerospace power, air war and management within the Air Force, through the Aerospace Policy and Strategy Program (CPEA) and the Command and Staff Program (CCEM

[1296] Air Force Academy - Pirassununga (State of São Paulo) Air Force Junior Officer School (EAOAR

[1297] The Air Force Junior Officer School was established in 1953, in Guarulhos (State of São Paulo), and later transferred to Rio de Janeiro

[1298] It has the mission of educating and training junior officers, with the aim of developing specific competencies for the exercise of administrative, advisory and operational roles, through the Air Force Junior Officer Program (CAP

[1299] Technological Institute of Aeronautics (ITA) The Technological Institute of Aeronautics was established in 1950 and is located in São Jose dos Campos (State of São Paulo

[1300] It is a university-level institution specialized in the field of aerospace and managed by the Air Force

[1301] It has the purpose of promoting progress in science and technology related to the field of aerospace through education, training and research

[1302] In particular, the Institute is in charge of providing education to graduates in key areas of interest for the Air Force and the aerospace sector in general

[1303] The institute is subordinated to the Department of Aerospace Science and Technology (DCTA

[1304] It is an essential element for the development of the scientific-technological aerospace sector

[1305] The excellence of the programs offered by ITA is evidenced by the exceptional results which this institution regularly achieves in the Courses General Index (IGC

[1306] It is consistently ranked one of the six best out of over two thousand assessed institutions

[1307] Institute's plans to double its number of undergraduate students within six years, from 120 to 240 students by

[1308] It also intends to expand its number of graduate students by 60% within the next five years

[1309] Air Force Training Center (CIAAR) This Center was established in 1983, replacing the Air Force Enlisted Training Center (CIGAR

[1310] It is currently being transferred from Pampulha, in Belo Horizonte (State of Minas Gerais), to Lagoa Santa (State of Minas Gerais

[1311] It is responsible for training civilians and military personnel for future employment as Air Force officers

[1312] Air Force Sergeant School (EEAR

[1313] The Air Force Sergeant School was created in 1941 through the merger of human and physical capital from the Navy and Army aviations

[1314] The Sergeant School was initially located in the Ponta do Galeão, Governor’s Island, in the city of Rio de Janeiro

[1315] In 1950, it was transferred to Guaratinguetá (State of São Paulo) where it remains to this day

[1316] This School is charged with developing Air Force sergeants

[1317] Scientific and Technological Institutions: Aeronautics and Space Institute (IAE

[1318] The Aeronautics and Space Institute was created from the merger, in 1991, of the Research and Development Institute (IPD) and the Space Activities Institute (IAE

[1319] Its mission is to conduct research and development in the field of aerospace

[1320] This Institute has been developing scientific and technological solutions to strengthen Brazilian Aerospace Power

[1321] In particular, it manages the satellite launching vehicle project, the sounding rockets project and the unmanned aerial vehicle project, among others

[1322] The Advanced Studies Institute originated from the fusion of the Advanced Studies Laboratory and the Advanced Studies Division, both belonging to the now extinguished Aerospace Technical Center (CTA

[1323] With the activation of the Technical Aerospace Command, the Advanced Studies Institute became a military organization of the Air Force, specifically assigned to the development of experimental technologies and the study of liquid propellants and supersonic combustion technology, among other affairs

[1324] Industrial Promotion and Coordination Institute (IFI

[1325] The Industrial Promotion and Coordination Institute was established in

[1326] Its mission is to contribute to the performance, security and availability of aerospace products and systems that are of interest to the Air Force

[1327] It renders services in the areas of legislation, metrology, certification, intellectual property, technological transference and industrial coordination

[1328] Thus, this Institute promotes the Brazilian scientific-technological aerospace industry

[1329] The Institute is recognized by the International Accreditation Forum (IAF) as an organization that certifies quality management systems and aerospace quality management systems

[1330] Foreign Exchange and International Cooperation The Air Force maintains a series of exchange and cooperation activities with foreign nations, especially with South American and African countries

[1331] The Brazilian Air Force Technical Mission (MTAB) in Paraguay, established in 1982 is noteworthy

[1332] Its objective is to supply technical, operational and administrative assistance to the Air Force of said country

[1333] The most relevant areas of exchange and cooperation are noted below: Military Education Foreign military personnel in Brazil

[1347] Women in the Air Force The Air Force created the Air Force Women's Reserve Corps (CFRA) in

[1348] In the following year the first cohort of this Corps was incorporated in Campo dos Afonsos, Rio de Janeiro

[1349] On that occasion, 150 women were admitted to the Air Force — psychologists, nurses, systems analysts, social assistants, phono-audiologists, nutritionists and librarians — selected from across the country

[1350] Women, therefore, have been part of the administrative life of the Air Force for three decades

[1351] In the Air Force Academy — where the military personnel that are allowed to achieve the highest ranks of the Air Force are trained and educated — women began to be admitted in 1996, in the Logistics Officer Program

[1352] In 2006, the Air Force graduated its first cohort of female military pilots

[1353] Female Air Force officers of the aviation, logistics, infantry, health and aeronautics engineering careers are allowed to be promoted to General Officer ranks, while competing in equal conditions for promotions with male officers

[1354] Currently, the Brazilian Air Force employs approximately four thousand women

[1355] Fighter Aircraft Pilot A

[1356] Super Tucano) The development and employment of military power is executed according to a Defense Structure, as provided in Decree No 7,276 of August

[1357] The Defense Structure is composed of the following officials: I. President of the Republic; II

[1358] Minister of Defense

[1359] Defense Council

[1360] Armed Forces Chiefs of Staff; V. Chief of the Joint Staff; and VI

[1361] Commanders of Operational Commands

[1362] The President of the Republic, as commander-in-chief of the Armed Forces, assisted by the Minister of Defense, and by the Defense Council, is responsible for the employment of military resources and for the strategic direction of the Armed Forces, as provided in Article 2 of Complementary Law No 97 of June

[1363] The Armed Forces have their own structure and are subordinated to the Minister of Defense

[1364] The Joint Staff (EMCFA) is the permanent advisory institution of the Minister of Defense for the direction of the Armed Forces

[1365] The Joint Staff prepares and coordinates the planning of joint employment in operations that involve military personnel and resources

[1366] In face of crisis or armed conflict, the President of the Republic approves a strategic plan for employment and appoints Commanders of Operational Commands

[1367] Once activated, each Operational Command is responsible for a geographical area — Theater of Operations — where the Commander has the authority to conduct military operations in order to reach selected and approved objectives

[1368] The Operational Commanders present to the Minister of Defense an overview of operations to be conducted and a proposal for the allocation of resources to their respective Commands

[1369] The Armed Forces Chiefs of Staff supply the resources allocated by the Minister of Defense to the Operational Commanders and provide the necessary logistical support

[1370] Joint Operations Doctrine The development of the Armed Forces, based on their operational capacities, allows the Ministry of Defense to possess military forces that are able to act jointly, with flexibility, versatility and mobility

[1371] Military operations of extended scope demand the employment of considerable resources from more than one Armed Service

[1372] To meet this challenge, the Armed Forces must join efforts, adopt compatible procedures and integrate actions in order to obtain greater efficiency and effectiveness in the execution of joint operations

[1373] The Joint Staff prepares the Strategic Plans for the Joint Employment of the Armed Forces (PECFA) and coordinates joint Armed Forces training and employment

[1374] The planning of a joint operation, though similar to any other operation, is distinct in terms of the heterogeneity of employment procedures and of the technical peculiarities of the participating Forces

[1375] Hence the importance of coordination and integration of planned actions through a joint operations doctrine

[1376] The joint operations doctrine sets conditions for the synchronization of naval, land and aerial actions, in order to attain strategic and operational objectives, in accordance with political, diplomatic and economic efforts

[1377] The goal is to be effective through the synergy of Force Components within the Operational Command operating in a certain Theater of Operations

[1378] Theater of Operations (TO) The Theater of Operations can be on land or sea and is comprised of the necessary area for operations to be conducted, including the respective logistical support

[1379] The Armed Forces that act in a TO are under a single Operational Command

[1380] The single Command allows the coordination of actions and greater interoperability among Forces, avoiding duplicate efforts and favoring the reduction of losses

[1381] An Operational Command will be of a joint nature whenever it is formed by more than one Force

[1382] On the other hand, each Force Component, integrating a Joint Operational Command, may be either of singular or joint nature

[1383] A Force Component will be of a singular nature whenever it is constituted of resources from just one Force

[1384] In this case the Force Component will receive the name Naval Force Component, Land Force Component or Air Force Component

[1385] The Component Force will have a joint nature whenever it incorporates considerable amounts of resources allocated from more than one Armed Force, under a single command

[1386] In this case, they may be called Joint Task Force (FT Cj) or Joint Force (F Cj), for example: Special Operations Joint Force (F Cj Op Esp), Logistic Command (C Log) etc

[1387] These military Forces must share areas of operation and carry out actions with a single objective, which requires a high degree of interoperability

[1388] This requires mutual knowledge and exchange of information and services regarding: employed forces; common procedures; standardization of plans and document; and equipment employed

[1389] Interoperability in Joint Operations Several Force Components coexist in the Theater of Operations, sharing the sea, land and aerial environments in a coordinated way, conducting actions for the achievement of missions

[1390] The Theater of Operations Command synchronizes the Force Components in order to reach strategic and operational objectives

[1391] The goal is to obtain effectiveness through the synergy of the various resources in the Theater

[1392] Esp FT Cj Bravo Joint Staff Theater of Operations Commander Air Force Component Land Force Component Naval Force Component Logistics Command F Cj Alfa Armed Forces in a Theater of Operations (TO) The Armed Forces Ensuring Law and Order (GLO) Law and order operations demand special preparation and training

[1393] The employment of the Armed Services in this type of operation is fundamentally different, in principles and doctrine, from their traditional employment in missions related to defense against foreign threats

[1394] In the latter, action is focused on enemy forces that are perfectly identifiable in the field and usually characterized by conventional military weaponry and uniforms

[1395] The peculiarities of this type of action has fostered the creation of a school for the study and research on the field

[1396] The Law and Order Operations Training Center (CIOpGLO), in Campinas (State of São Paulo), subordinated to the 11th Light Infantry Brigade, seeks to modernize doctrine and the development of human resources for this type of employment

[1397] The employment of the Armed Forces in law and order operations is the prerogative of the President of the Republic as provided in Article 15 of Complementary Law

[1398] No 97 of

[1399] The Law specifies that following presidential orders, the Armed Forces’ operational units will be activated to carry out the preventive and repressive actions necessary to ensure the expected results, in an episodic manner, within a previously established area and for a limited time

[1400] Altered by Complementary Laws No. 117 of 2004 and No. 136 of

[1401] COMMAND AND CONTROL INTEROPERABILITY Army AAAE CINDACTA Remote Station Air Base ComOpNav Navy Remote Station Interoperability After the decision to employ the Armed Forces to guarantee law and order, it is the duty of the competent authority (e.g. state Governor) to formally transfer the operational control of necessary law enforcement agencies to the authority in charge of the operations

[1402] The latter should establish an operations coordination center, comprised of representatives of the public agencies under his operational control or with related interests

[1403] Brazilian legislation highlights the subsidiary nature of the Armed Forces’ intervention, which should only occur “when all other means assigned to the preservation of public order and the safety of the people and their property are exhausted”, as determined in Article 144 of the Federal Constitution

[1404] Decree No. 3,897 of 2001 determines that public security means are considered exhausted whenever, within a certain period of time, they become unavailable, inexistent or insufficient for the regular performance of their constitutional mission

[1405] It is also noteworthy that -- as provided in Article 5 of same decree -- the Armed Forces may be employed to ensure law and order in situations when disturbances are presumable, such as in official and public events, particularly those in which heads of states or of foreign governments participate, or on elections, upon request by the Electoral High Court

[1406] Usually, the Armed Forces are employed to ensure law and order jointly with federal, state and municipal security agencies

[1407] Brazil’s Participation in the World Wars Brazil’s participation in the First World War was motivated by a series of episodes involving Brazilian ships in Europe

[1408] For example, in April of 1917, the merchant vessel “Paraná” was sunk near the English Channel

[1409] During the following months, other Brazilian merchant vessels were sunk as well

[1410] President Wenceslau Bras made an alliance with the countries of the Triple Entente and, even without substantial military power, Brazil supported them, logistically, with agricultural and raw materials

[1411] In compliance with commitments to the Inter-allied Conference, the Brazilian government sent a medical mission comprised of civilian and military surgeons, to act in campaign hospitals in the European Theater of Operations, a number of aviation officers, from the Army and the Navy, to join the Allied Air Force and, also, part of the Fleet, essentially to conduct anti-submarine warfare

[1412] In order to meet the Navy’s duties, the organization of a task force named “Naval Division for Operations of War” (DNOG) was established

[1413] It was constituted of ships from the Brazilian Fleet divisions

[1414] The following vessels joined the DNOG: the cruisers “Rio Grande do Sul” and “Bahia”, the destroyers “Piauí”, “Rio Grande do Norte”, “Paraíba” and “Santa Catarina”, the supply ship “Belmonte” and the tugboat “Laurindo Pitta”

[1415] The total naval contingent was approximately 1.5 thousand men

[1416] The division was charged with patrolling an area within a maritime triangle, which extended from the city of Dakar, in the African coast to the island of São Vicente, in the Cape Verde archipelago, and Gibraltar, in the Mediterranean

[1417] The participation of Brazil in World War II was also strongly motivated by the successive sinking of Brazilian merchant vessels

[1418] In reaction to popular pressure, Brazil declared war on the Axis powers in August

[1419] The participation of the country in this conflict was more significant than during the First World War

[1420] Brazilian operations in the South Atlantic started almost at once

[1421] The Navy controlled the navigation routes between North and South America

[1422] It also participated in joint and combined operations, including the air-naval patrol, protection and escort of naval convoys and preventive actions to impede attacks by the Axis powers on Brazilian soil

[1423] The Brazilian Merchant ship “Buarque” torpedoed — 1942 Naval War ship confronting a submarine The Navy was the only South American naval force that took part in the two world wars

[1424] It protected the country’s international trade and ensured necessary supplies for the nation

[1425] The Army sent approximately 25 thousand men to fight in Europe against the Axis powers

[1426] The Brazilian Expeditionary Force (FEB) integrated the Fifth US Army

[1427] After heavy losses, the FEB’s first successful operation was the siege of Monte Castello Fortress, which was occupied and garrisoned by a highly capable enemy division

[1428] Following this success, the FEB carried out its subsequent missions with equally impressive results

[1429] The first enemy force to surrender in Italy did so to FEB personnel, the so-called “Smoking Snakes”

[1430] During the World War II Italian campaign, the FEB took custody of over 20 thousand prisoners

[1431] The Brazilian Air Force was created during World War II, in

[1432] In 1942, it began to carry out engagements in the patrol of Brazilian waters

[1433] In 1943, the 1st Fighter Group was created

[1434] In the same year, a Brazilian Air Force aircraft sank an Axis submarine off the coast of Rio de Janeiro

[1435] The 1st Fighter Group operated in Italy from 1944 and integrated the 350th US Fighter Group

[1436] It executed over two thousand missions and distinguished itself as one of three foreign air units to be awarded a United States Presidential Unit Citation

[1437] The Brazilian Expeditionary Force adopted the smoking snake as its symbol due to what was said at the time: that it would be easier to see a snake smoking than to see Brazil at war

[1438] Also worth noting is the participation in Italy of the 1st Liaison and Observation Squadron, which acted jointly with the Divisionary Artillery, both units of the Brazilian Expeditionary Force

[1439] In this joint action, the aircraft crews were formed by Air Force pilots and by Army air observers, whose task it was to regulate artillery fire

[1440] Brazil’s Participation in Peacekeeping Missions Since 1947, the country participates in United Nations peacekeeping missions, having already contributed with over 30 thousand military and police personnel

[1441] It has integrated operations in Africa (Congo, Angola, Mozambique, Liberia, Uganda and Sudan), in Central America and the Caribbean (El Salvador, Nicaragua, Guatemala and Haiti), in Asia (Cambodia and East Timor) and in Europe (Cyprus and Croatia

[1442] Brazil assigned troops to Suez (UNEF), Angola (UNAVEM), Mozambique (ONUMOZ), East Timor (UNTAET/UNMISET), Haiti (MINUSTAH) and, recently, in Lebanon (UNIFIL

[1443] An important example of Brazilian participation in peacekeeping missions is the presence of a contingent of the Armed Forces in Haiti, since 2004, in the United Nations Stabilizing Mission — MINUSTAH

[1444] Brazil supplies over two thousand Army, Navy and Air Force military personnel, the largest contingent among the 15 participating countries and holds the command of this Peacekeeping Force

[1445] The MINUSTAH was created by resolution of the United Nations Security Council

[1446] It contributes to the reestablishment of institutional normality in Haiti after a long period of political instability

[1447] The MINUSTAH’s role in ensuring the presidential elections of 2006 and 2010, with peaceful transition of power, is noteworthy

[1448] As is the relevant role of the United Nations Mission in reconstructing Haiti after the severe earthquake of January

[1449] The work undertaken by the Brazilian military is acknowledged by the Haitian people and by international authorities

[1450] This is due to the military’s particular form of action, a result of prior training received and its own professional expertise acquired with time

[1451] The Haiti mission also allows participating Forces to improve their operational and logistical systems, as well as maintain exchanges with troops from other nations

[1452] In coordination with the UN and other UNASUL countries which integrate the Mission, Brazil plans a gradual withdrawal of its troops from Haiti, in accordance with the desire and capacity of the Haitian government to guarantee the country’s security

[1453] Another recent example of Brazil’s participation in peacekeeping missions is the Brazilian presence in the United Nations Interim Force in Lebanon (UNIFIL

[1454] In February 2011, a Brazilian Admiral took office as Commander of the Maritime Task Force (MTF) of the UNIFIL

[1455] The MTF was established in 2006 and is the first Naval Task Force to integrate a UN Peacekeeping Mission

[1456] It is assigned to prevent the entrance of illegal weapons into Lebanon, as well as to train the Lebanese Navy’s personnel

[1457] The Frigate “União” was incorporated into the MTF in November 2011 and replaced by Frigate “Liberal” in May

[1458] The UNIFIL was created in 1978, with the purpose of keeping stability in the region, during the Israeli troops’ withdrawal from Lebanese territory

[1464] Armed Forces’ Role in Ensuring Law and Order The participation of the Brazilian military in a Pacification Force for the Complexo do Alemão and Complexo da Penha — both low income suburban neighborhoods of the city of Rio de Janeiro — is another example of the Armed Forces’ work aiming to fulfill their constitutional mission

[1465] This Pacification Force was created by the Ministry of Defense in 2010, by order of the President of the Republic, in order to cooperate with the Government of the State of Rio de Janeiro in the establishment of law and order, following illegalities committed by organized crime

[1466] The “Alemão-Penha” neighborhoods were occupied following coordinated action by the Army, Navy and the State of Rio de Janeiro’s Military Police Force

[1467] The Pacification Force, established under the authority of the Army’s Eastern Command, acted to ensure the safety of the population until police personnel could be prepared to occupy Police Pacification Units (UPP) in the communities

[1468] Besides these operations, the military also undertook civic and social activities (ACISO), with the aim of improving the quality of life of over 400 thousand dwellers in those neighborhoods

[1469] Concept in detail in Chapter

[1470] Pacification Force Data Force Quantity of military personnel Expenditures (R$ Millions) 2011 2012 Total Navy 148 5,4 - 5,4 Army 1,800 198 135,4 333,4 The Ágata Operations are another recent example of Federal troops ensuring Law and Order

[1471] These operations are conducted in the borderlands, in partnership with the Ministries of Justice and Finance

[1472] They are joint activities conducted by the Brazilian Armed Forces and they aim to combat trans-border and environmental crimes, in coordination with other federal and state agencies

[1473] The operations are prepared in accordance with the Strategic Border Plan, instated by Decree No 7,496 of June

[1474] The main objectives of this Plan are to: neutralize organized crime, reduce crime levels, cooperate with neighboring countries and support the local population

[1475] In 2011, Operations Agata-1, in the State of Amazonas, Agata-2, in the States of Rio Grande do Sul, Paraná and Santa Catarina and Agata-3 in the States of Mato Grosso do Sul, Mato Grosso, Rondonia, Acre and Amazonas were carried out

[1476] In 2012, in continuation, Operation Agata-4 took place in the States of Amapá, Pará, Roraima and Amazonas
